No clear difference in these light/short loads; heavy sustained animation (apps, scrolling) needs someone using the phone. A few frames still hit the 3-vsync limit (the GPU really took >50 ms on frames that refill full-screen surfaces). Left kern.sweetfb_gpuwait=0 (the default). To try it: sysctl kern.sweetfb_gpuwait=1 + kickstart backboardd, then compare kern.sweetfb_shown per second while scrolling.
  - MDP SCANOUT RESEARCH (offline, untested): docs/SUNFISH-MDP-SCANOUT.md. Display fetch addresses are 32-bit and XNU RAM is above 4 GiB, so direct scanout of IOSurfaces needs an SMMU stage-1 context for MDP stream 0x800 (or screen surfaces placed inside the 0x9c000000 splash carve-out, which fits two 4608x2436 buffers). Crop needs no scaler (SRC_XY/SRC_SIZE, DMA pipe ok); fit needs VIG0/1 QSEED3LITE (bilinear, no LUTs; values in the doc). Changes latch with CTL_FLUSH, but the downstream driver never flushes during autorefresh. Unknown: which pipe ABL uses, the 0x800 S2CR. Order: read-only probe -> flip SRC0_ADDR within the carve-out -> SMMU -> scaler. Riskier than the GPU-to-panel plan above, which reuses the GPU's existing SMMU: do that first.
  - BRIGHTNESS RESEARCH (offline, from EvolutionX msm-4.14 sources, untested): docs/SUNFISH-BACKLIGHT.md. No PWM/WLED: DCS 0x51 with a 10-bit value (4..1023, high byte first; 0x53=0x20 already sent by ABL's on-sequence). Send from XNU via the DSI0 (0x0AE94000) command FIFO path (no DMA buffer/SMMU needed); Linux does not pause the display for it. Plan: a small sunfish-only kernel driver behind a lock, hooked to iOS's backlight interface.
  - RESEARCH (offline, 2026-10-27): every frame is copied TWICE. (1) eagl target_to_surface(): the GPU blits CoreAnimation's frame (Mesa's own texture) into the screen IOSurface's memory ('mirror' texture, glBlitFramebuffer, GL_NEAREST); (2) the kernel's vsync present() copies that surface into the panel memory 0x9c000000 on the CPU (10 MB, and with fit the 24/25 shrink). PLAN "GPU to panel": make the panel memory a GPU target too (it is already IOSurface 1, sweet_surface_create_physical, which sweet-shot reads) and let eagl's existing end-of-frame blit also write it, scaled 1125x2436 -> 1080x2338 at y=1 with GL_LINEAR (free on the GPU, better than the CPU's 13th+14th average). The kernel then skips present()'s copy for a swap whose layer-0 surface eagl marked as already on the panel (eagl sets an IOSurface value, e.g. SweetOnPanel = frame number; SweetSurface keeps values since 0124; the kernel compares), and copies as now for anything else (layers eagl did not draw). Steps: a) time present() first (mach_absolute_time around the copy, a sysctl): proves whether the CPU copy is the ~46 fps limit; b) eagl: IOSurfaceLookup(1) as a second mirror, blit scaled when kern.sweetfb_fit; c) kernel skip. Open questions: can backboardd (mobile) look up surface 1; does Mesa import the physical surface (sweet_gpu_map with phys exists); tearing is the same as now (neither waits for the panel's TE). Longer term: scan out straight from the surfaces (MDP SSPP_SRC0_ADDR, needs physically contiguous screen surfaces or an SMMU mapping for the MDP; fit would need a VIG pipe's QSEED3 scaler) - more risk, no copy at all.
  - xnu 0145 FIT: sysctl kern.sweetfb_fit / boot-arg sweetfb_fit=1 shrinks the presented 1125x2436 by 24/25 onto the 1080x2340 panel, whole (1080x2338, 1 black row top and bottom; of each 25 columns/rows the 13th+14th averaged, rest copied), touch mapped the same (SweetHID.cpp). Default OFF. Image shim/out/shim-local-0145.img BOOTED: screenshots fit0/fit1 (scratchpad shots/fit-compare.png): whole picture visible (battery, home indicator), text sharp. NOT YET TESTED: touch accuracy with the user, frame-rate cost while scrolling. If both are fine: make it the default (sweetfb_fit=1 in the cmdline, or default 1 in start()). User wants it on: standard image is now shim/out/shim-local-0145-fit.img (current tree incl. Wi-Fi 0138, default cmdline + sweetfb_fit=1, BOOTED, kern.sweetfb_fit=1); the Wi-Fi session added sweetfb_fit=1 to its cmdlines. FRAME RATE (user scrolling Settings, kern.sweetfb_shown): 0145 fit on 28.2 fps vs fit off 46.0 (a pixel at a time into the panel); xnu 0146 copies runs (12 + mix + 11 per 25, memcpy; mixed rows via a line buffer): fit on 40.7 fps, ~2 min after boot (fresh boots read low). Standard image now shim/out/shim-local-0146-fit.img (BOOTED). Not working yet, no drivers (user, 2026-10-27): brightness (backlight), sound, microphone. Pixel 4a front camera hole (Android overlay config_mainBuiltInDisplayCutout): circle r=42 centred (83,83) px, i.e. x/y 41..125: it sits on iOS's status bar clock (left ear) in both modes; not handled yet.
  - BLUR A/B (user scrolling Settings, 0144 boot, kern.sweetfb_shown, scratchpad sample-blur.txt): blur on 37.1 fps (first round, minutes after boot), Reduce Transparency on 46.6 fps, blur on again 46.5 fps. Blur is NOT the limit; the first round was slow because the boot was fresh. Scrolling sits at ~46 fps (of 60) whatever the GPU wait or blur. Next suspect: the per-frame 10 MB CPU copy into the panel at the vsync tick (present()), then CoreAnimation's own CPU time.
  - GREEN-YELLOW STRIP at the bottom of the verbose boot (user photo 2026-10-27): it is sweet_early_mark()'s stage bar (osfmk/arm64/sweet_early.c, 48-px blocks per stage, colour green + (stage&7)*32 red); the video console's 16-px rows stop 4 px short of 2340. xnu 0144 blacks out the bar in sweet_early_disable(). Image shim/out/shim-local-0144.img (default cmdline, includes the Wi-Fi session's 0137) BOOTED to iOS; user to confirm the strip is gone.
  - Root rebuild: tools/sweet-root-tweaks.py ROOT --gpu MESA_OUT now installs the GPU stack (libOSMesa.8.dylib, libsunfish-gl.dylib, libsunfish-inject.dylib in /usr/lib; bbd_wrap as /usr/libexec/backboardd, original moved to /usr/libexec/real/backboardd), as it is on the phone. Tested on a fake root only. Still NOT scripted (done by hand on the phone): jailbreak/bootstrap (tools/jb-to-root.sh), sweet-net, the 32 staged apps' later state, the Wi-Fi session's files. Before wiping userdata for Android: pull a fresh full userdata image (logs/phone-root.img is from 10-26 and predates the GPU stack).
  - USER A/B on 0143 (user scrolling Settings ~20 s each, kern.sweetfb_shown sampled each second, scratchpad sample.txt): gpuwait=0 46.2 fps (51 s, 0 late), gpuwait=1 43.9 fps (41 s, 3 late). No gain: drawing waiting for the GPU is not what limits scrolling. Left gpuwait=0. Next perf work should find what does limit it at ~46 fps (the 11 MB CPU copy into the panel per frame at vsync? eagl's surface hashing/refills?), measured with kern.sweetfb_shown.

- User chose the full path. Facts: sunfish Wi-Fi = WCN3990 (ath10k snoc); its firmware (wlanmdsp.mbn) runs as a protection domain on the MODEM (MPSS), so MPSS must boot first. Android side: remoteproc q6v5_mss + tqftpserv (serves wlanmdsp.mbn) + rmtfs + pd-mapper + qrtr, then ath10k talks WLFW/QMI over QRTR (SUNFISH_PROGRESS.md 2026-10-08).
- Local firmware (untracked, research/ is git-ignored): research/fw-local/image/modem.* (mdt + b00-b30, from ~/sunfishbackupp/part/modem_a.img, read-only mcopy), qdsp6m.qdb, research/fw-local/wlanmdsp.mbn (from ~/evox17 vendor/firmware). The MBA image (needed by q6v5_mss on SM7150) was NOT found in modem_a or evox17 vendor/firmware: find it (xbl/imagefv/other partition or the stock A-image firmware) before the MPSS step.

Root cause of -3145799: TrustZone takes the metadata buffer as read/write (QCOM_SCM_RW) during PAS_INIT_IMAGE and clobbers it; in 0130/0131 mdt pointed directly to that buffer, so segment.type read as 0 after init_image, skipping all segments (0 segments copied; first-word probe stayed 0; TrustZone authenticated empty RAM). Fix (0132): cache phdrs in static mpss_phdrs[64] before init_image, pass a separate page to TrustZone, copy all 26 file segments and zero remainder, optimized 64-bit zeroing in mpss_write. Result in live log: "SWEET MPSS: alive 30 s after the start: [31 segs 0x8b000000..0x94800000][rails 0][crypto 0 ddr 0][0x6 -> 0,0][0x1 -> 0,0][0x2 -> 0,0][first word before 0][26 segments copied, first word after 0x5800c0005800c00e][0x5 -> 0,0]". AUTH_AND_RESET(4) = 0! Next: Step 3 (SMEM + SMP2P in XNU to catch the modem ready/handover interrupt and signal, then GLINK/QRTR).

## 0110: ios-topaz zap_load (their 0901, works on SM6225) rounds each segment end up to 4 KB: PIL_MEM_SETUP(13, region, 0x1000). Ours (and downstream/mainline arithmetic) passed 0x7c0. 0110 rounds up. Test with adreno_zap_fresh=1 adreno_skip=4096.

## 0109 result (logs/live-0109.txt): metadata at 0xb0000000 (below 4 GiB), init_image 0, PAS_MEM_SETUP still -3145798. So: not args, not bw vote, not order, not metadata address. adreno_trust blocked.

## 0108 result: same -3145798 with the downstream order (no step 12, no shutdown). Peer session (mainline kernel, Driver port 4.14 to 7.1): the GPU NEVER worked on mainline sunfish (a630_sqe.fw missing; the zap shader was never attempted; no KGSL port). Its lead: Linux metadata is below 4 GiB; some QTI TZ reject >4 GiB. 0109: adreno_zap_meta=0xb0000000 (free low RAM, our non-highmem base).

## adreno_trust=1 result (logs/live-0107t.txt, 01:45-01:52): the GPU thread stops at "about to write gpu rbbm_secvid_trust_cntl at 0x503d000: 0" and never returns; iOS keeps running (ping, log). The secure world blocks that register on sunfish (as linux a6xx_gpu.c warns). So secure mode can only be left through the zap shader: 0108 test next (adreno_zap_fresh=1 adreno_skip=4096).

## 0108: downstream msm-4.14 (subsys-pil-tz.c pil_init_image_trusted / pil_mem_setup_trusted, peripheral-loader.c pil_boot) makes the same PAS_MEM_SETUP(13, region, max-min) call; mem_setup has no bw vote there (only init_image/auth). Difference is the sequence: downstream init_image -> mem_setup -> load -> auth once; ours step 12 init_image (dangling) then step 19 shutdown + init_image + mem_setup. 0108 adreno_zap_fresh=1 skips the shutdown; test with adreno_skip=4096 (no step 12).

## 0107 result (logs/live-0107.txt, 01:13): crypto vote ok (CE0 0x500a0, SN11 0x5006c, SN0 0x50078 + DDR vote) but PAS_MEM_SETUP(13, 0x98f95000, 0x7c0) still -3145798; no zap shader; gputest still all zeros. So the bandwidth vote was not it. Next ideas: (a) boot-arg adreno_trust=1 (0106) to leave secure mode by register; (b) find what -3145798 means and how Linux/the mainline port actually loads the zap shader on this phone (ask the user for the mainline session; check whether the mainline GPU really comes up with zap); (c) compare with topaz 0901-0947.

## 0107 (mainline comparison, as the user asked): mainline sunfish dts has a zap shader (gpu_mem 0x98f95000, 8 KB, same as ours; a615_zap.elf as .mbn), so TZ loads it for Linux. Our step 19: PAS_INIT_IMAGE ok, PAS_MEM_SETUP(13, 0x98f95000, 0x7c0) -> SMC returns -3145798. Same arguments as linux (zap segment is RELOCATABLE, paddr 0x5000, 0x7c0 bytes; mdt_loader.c calls mem_setup with max-min). Difference: linux qcom_scm.c wraps every PAS call in qcom_scm_bw_enable() = icc_set_bw(scm path, 0, UINT_MAX), path MASTER_CRYPTO_CORE_0 -> SLAVE_EBI_CH0 = BCMs CE0 -> SN11 -> SN0 -> SH0 -> MC0/ACV (drivers/interconnect/qcom/sm7150.c). 0107 votes CE0/SN11/SN0 (new sweet_rpmh_crypto_vote) + MC0/SH0/ACV (sweet_rpmh_ddr_vote) before the zap load. If the zap shader loads, step 21 leaves secure mode with CP_SET_SECURE_MODE and adreno_trust is not needed.

## 0105 result (logs/live-0105.txt, 00:55): Mesa's 2 submissions run to the end: ring read = written, IB 0 words to go, CP fault 0, protection 0, fence advanced, no SMMU fault reported; pixels still 0. Step 20 already sets all 12 ADDR_MODE_CNTL to 64-bit (checked). Remaining difference to linux hw_init: no zap shader (TZ refuses the load), and we never leave secure mode; linux writes RBBM_SECVID_TRUST_CNTL=0 then (a6xx_gpu.c, with a warning that the write is blocked where a zap shader is required). 0106: that write behind boot-arg adreno_trust=1.

Options when it matters: (1) rebuild with mkiosroot.sh at partition size minus a reserve for the log ring (last 4 MiB + 4 KiB) and send only the data extents over adb; (2) grow in place with XNU hfs_extendfs from the kernel, after the UFS driver reports the partition minus the log area (today DKIOCGETBLOCKCOUNT covers the whole partition, so any grow to the device size would overwrite the log).

## 0098, 2026-10-25: boot with 0097 (photo): `SWEET UFS init failed: error 5; hce 0x1 hcs 0x10f is 0x0 cap 0x1587031f utrlba 0x1:4570000 rsr 0x1 dbr 0x0; last cmd 0x1b ocs 0x0 upiu 0x21 resp 0x1 status 0x2 sense key 0x2 asc 0x4 ascq 0x2`. Observed: LUN 0 NOT READY, LOGICAL UNIT NOT READY, INITIALIZING COMMAND REQUIRED; even START STOP UNIT (start) to LUN 0 got the same. Reading (UFS spec, Linux ufshcd_set_dev_pwr_mode): device in PowerDown/Sleep, only the UFS Device W-LU (UPIU LUN 0xD0) takes SSU with POWER CONDITION Active (cdb[4] = 0x10). 0098 does that, matching the Pixel kernel (android_kernel_google_msm-4.14 ufshcd_set_dev_pwr_mode: REQUEST SENSE 18 bytes to the W-LU first, then SSU), panic now also prints the LUN. Not yet tested on hardware.

## 0097, 2026-10-25: first sunfish boot with 0096, photographed panic: `SWEET UFS init failed: error 5; hce 0x1 hcs 0x10f is 0x0 cap 0x1587031f utlba 0x1:4538000 rsr 0x1 dbr 0x0; last cmd 0x00 ocs 0x0 upiu 0x21 resp 0x1 status 0x2; dma 0x104538000`. Observations: host enabled and ready, request list accepted, TEST UNIT READY (0x00) completes at transport level and the unit answers CHECK CONDITION (status 2) on all 3 tries. Sense key unknown (the driver did not read it). Guess, not verified: unit left not started / unit attention by the Pixel bootloader. 0097 reads the sense data, sends START STOP UNIT on key 2, retries 10x with 50 ms waits, and prints key/asc/ascq in the panic.

## >>> 0087, 2026-10-23: DDR bandwidth vote + battery temperature (docs/SUNFISH.md) <<<

sweet_rpmh.c refactored: rh_lookup(res, type) for VRM and BCM, rh_send(addresses, values, n, what) generic (L11C goes through it unchanged in values; conflict check now per 4-byte address). New sweet_rpmh_ddr_vote(): HackiOS ddr_raise() on SM7150: BCM MC0/SH0 vote(x=y=16383), ACV vote(0, 8). Called from sweet_cpufreq_init (`ddr` boot-arg, default 2 with cpufreq=1). sweet_spmi_battery_temp(): PM6150 ADC5 (0x3100) BAT_THERM_100K_PU 0x4a one conversion (0x42..0x46 then CONV_REQ 0x47, STATUS1 EOC, DATA0/1, 1.875 V / 0x70e4, 100k NTC table); sweet_spmi_write allow-list extended to 0x3142..0x3147. Thermal service reads it once a second (off-table readings ignored). Not run on hardware; the sweet L11C path changed by the refactor is not re-tested either.

## >>> 0086, 2026-10-23: CPU speed (OSM) + thermal caps (docs/SUNFISH.md) <<<

`bsd/dev/sweet_cpufreq.c`, ported from HackiOS hk_cpufreq.c (their EPSS -> SM7150 OSM, qcom-cpufreq-hw sdm845 regs): domains 0x18323000 (cpu0-5) / 0x18325800 (cpu6-7); enable 0x0 bit0, LUT 0x110+32n (src[31:30], cores[18:16], lval[7:0]), volt 0x114+32n, current 0x704, perf state 0x920. `cpufreq=2` read-only log, `cpufreq=1` top non-boost row for groups with our cores. sweet_thermal.c now caps raised groups and panics after 2 s past 110 C at lowest speed (HackiOS behaviour); without cpufreq=1 it stays watch-only. Battery temperature not wired (needs a PM6150 ADC5 driver). Haptics deferred: HackiOS feeds the motor from their CoreAudio "Actuator" plug-in (userland), not possible without an iOS toolchain here. Not run on hardware.

## >>> 0085, 2026-10-23: thermal service, watch-only (docs/SUNFISH.md) <<<

`bsd/dev/sweet_thermal.c` + `sweet_thermal.h`, ported from HackiOS `metal` (hk_thermal.c/.h, APSL 2.0): 25 TSENS v2 sensors (tsens0 TM 0xc263000 x15, tsens1 TM 0xc265000 x10; names from msm-4.14 sdmmagpie-thermal.dtsi), their governor unchanged with no caps (no cpufreq for OSM yet), sysctls kern.hackios.thermal* (their thermald's names). Default on for google,sunfish, `thermal=0` off, `thermal=2` any board. No emergency panic without caps (log only). Not run on hardware.

## >>> 0084, 2026-10-22: RTC + sunfish display fallback (docs/SUNFISH.md) <<<

SweetPlatformExpert publishes IORTC (IOKitInitializeTime() used to wait 30 s for it on every boot, then start the calendar at 1970) and answers getGMTTimeOfDay() from the PM6150 RTC (SPMI 0x6046/0x6048) + boot-arg `sweetrtc_offset`. AppleCLCD on google,sunfish: re-arms MDP pingpong autorefresh and, if it does not stick, writes CTL_START of the active CTL after each frame/fade step (`sweetfb_kick=0` disables). Charger state already exists behind `sweetbattery=1` (same PM6150). Not run on hardware.

## >>> SUNFISH (Pixel 4a) PORT, 2026-10-21 (read docs/SUNFISH.md) <<<

**State: code written, not booted on hardware.** Branch `claude/ios-sunfish-port-sunfish-uvpkdw`.
- Same SM7150 as sweet: CPUs, GIC, RAM layout (upper bank), splash FB `0x9c000000`, UFS, USB, SPMI/PMICs, keys and the touch I2C bus (SE7, reset 8, IRQ 9) are shared. One kernel for both phones.
- Differences handled: (1) the panel is 1080x2340 and **command mode**. `shim/disp.c` turns on MDP pingpong autorefresh so the framebuffer keeps refreshing (`DEVICE=sunfish shim/build.sh` builds with `-DFB_H=2340 -DCMD_MODE_PANEL` and the Pixel boot.img offsets). (2) The touch is an **ST FTM5 @0x49**. Patch 0082 adds an FTM5 path to `sweet_touch.c`, selected by ADT root compatible `google,sunfish` (`tools/mkadt.py --device sunfish`). Power comes from the PM6150 GPIO4 load switch through a new, address-restricted `sweet_spmi_write()`. (3) SweetHIDTouch takes the panel size from the boot framebuffer, and the platform expert also matches `google,sunfish`.
- Verified here: shim cross-builds for both devices, `mkadt.py --device sunfish` output, patch 0082 applies on top of 0001-0081 (local xnu-8796.141.3 tree). Kernel compile: see the CI run noted below (if any).
- Next on hardware: boot the payload-less shim first (does the red screen update? `DISP` lines), then the full payload. Checklist in docs/SUNFISH.md.

**Phone workflow (user does buttons, Claude runs commands):**
- Boot test kernel: phone in fastboot -> `fastboot boot shim/out/shim-boot-X.img`. NEVER `fastboot getvar`.
- **Read logs WITHOUT filming:** after a test the user holds Power+VolDown -> fastboot -> `fastboot boot recovery/recovery.img` (OrangeFox, temp) -> wait for `adb shell true` -> `tools/sweet-log-pull.sh /run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/logs/<name>.txt` (reads the 4 MiB console ring the kernel keeps in the last 1025 blocks of userdata; patch 0047). Then `adb reboot bootloader` brings the phone back to fastboot without the user.
- **Flash a root image:** `fastboot flash -S 512M userdata <img>.simg` (7 min, 25 chunks). After a big flash the Xiaomi ABL fastboot hangs: next `fastboot boot` stalls at "Sending" -> the USER must re-enter fastboot by hand (Power+VolDown). Do not retry commands against the hung ABL. A flash right after `adb reboot bootloader` once failed on chunk 1 (USB protocol error): just retry. User must NOT unplug during flash (once interrupted at 25/25 -> reflash).
- **Edit the phone root without reflashing (same-length only):** find the file's raw offset in the local image (it is one extent; search the image for its first 4 KiB), compare with `adb exec-out dd if=/dev/block/sda34 ...`, then `adb push` changed 4 KiB blocks and `dd of=/dev/block/sda34 seek=... conv=notrunc,fsync`, read back. Recovery kernel has no hfsplus.
- **Pull the whole phone root** (12 min): `adb exec-out "dd if=/dev/block/sda34 bs=1048576 count=16384" > img`; mount ro with udisksctl or `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,ro`. To write into a pulled image: `sudo fsck.hfsplus -fy` first (volume is never cleanly unmounted -> Linux mounts ro even with -o force), then `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,force`.
- Making images: copy latest img with `cp --sparse=always`, mount rw with force, change, `sync; umount`, `sha256sum`, `img2simg`. Keep only the latest image + one fallback on the SSD (/home is ~98% full; big work files live in `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/`, old kernels/images archived in `.../ios-sweet-work/archive/`).

**Diagnostics built into the kernel (all SWEET-only):** persistent console log (0047, boot-arg sweetlog=0 off; flush every ~1 s); `|name| text` copies of /dev/null writes for processes in `sweetnull` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0048); `IOK name:` IOKit lookup/open trace for `sweetiolog` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0049/0054, 400 lines max); `SWEET Sandbox: proc op N` (0050); `STR ...` heap text dump on SIGABRT/SIGTRAP for `sweetscan` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0051/0053) - this is how NSException/BSAssert reasons were found; `SMP name tN pc .. bt ..` user stack sampler for `sweetsample` (default SpringBoard,backboardd; first at 90 s then every 120 s, 40 samples, 16 frames; 0054/0055); `SWEET FB: N swaps` every 1000 swaps; `CRASH name ...` crash reports (0043/0044). Symbolicate cache addresses with `ipsw dyld a2s research/dsc/dyld_shared_cache_arm64 <addr - slide>`; main-binary addresses: addr - mh + 0x100000000.

- **v11 still migrated (boot 0056u, 20:32 UTC, `logs/boot-0056u-v11.txt`).** On iOS kCFPreferencesCurrentHost lands in the plain plist: all DMEnvironment setters (setLastBuildVersionPref, setUserDataDispositionPref, ...) use CurrentHost and the phone's `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` holds DMUserDataDisposition; datamigrator's XPC service runs with `_RoleAccount: mobile`. **Image v12** = v11 + `LastSystemVersion` = 20H392 added to that plain `com.apple.migration.plist` (keeps DMUserDataDisposition, 501:501 0600) + `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin` moved to `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL.disabled/` (Codex: stops the mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop; untested). **installcoordinationd loop explained (Codex `codex/installcoordinationd-report.md`):** os_crash "Failed to create data directory" EACCES for `/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/systemgroup.com.apple.installcoordinationd/Library/InstallCoordination`: `Library`/`Library/Caches` in the container are ACL-only (uid/gid 0xfffffffe, mode 0000, ACE for group GUID ...012B = gid 299 systemusers); with `config_ext_resolver` the kernel asks an identity resolver (memberd/opendirectoryd) that iOS does not run -> EWOULDBLOCK -> ACE skipped -> EACCES. **Patch 0057**: drop config_ext_resolver from FILESYS_BASE (compatibility UID/GID<->GUID mapping is used instead), likely fixes other ACL-protected containers too. Codex task 3: UFS driver performance design (`codex/ufs-performance.md`).
- **mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop explained (Codex report `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/codex/mediaserverd-report.md`):** the fault (FAR 0x390) is in the HAL plug-in `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin/VirtualAudio` (ApplyBeamFormerPreference <- VirtualAudio_PlugIn::SetPropertyData <- HALS_AHPPlugIn::ObjectSetPropertyData <- AudioObjectSetPropertyData <- MediaExperience vaemVADSerializationListener): the default virtual audio device ('vdef', VirtualAudioDevice_Default) does not exist (no audio hardware/driver, no /product/audio). Proposed workaround for GUI bring-up: move VirtualAudio.plugin out of /Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/ in the next root image (untested). v11 reflashed OK after an interrupted flash (USB unplugged at chunk 25/25). - **ROOT CAUSE of the endless migration: no IOPlatformUUID (2026-10-17).** datamigrator's `-[DMMigrator isMigrationNeededWithBuildVersion:lastBuildVersion:clientPID:]` gets lastBuildVersion from `-[DMEnvironment lastBuildVersionPref]` = `CFPreferencesCopyValue("LastSystemVersion", "com.apple.migration", kCFPreferencesCurrentUser, kCFPreferencesCurrentHost)` -> a ByHost file. The phone had `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` (only DMUserDataDisposition) and NO `Preferences/ByHost` directory at all. On arm XNU publishes IOPlatformUUID (gethostuuid) only from `/chosen/unique-chip-id` (8 bytes) + `/chosen/chip-id` (4 bytes) (IOPlatformExpertDevice::generatePlatformUUID, called from IOPlatformExpert::start); the SWEET ADT had neither, so every CurrentHost preference write failed. Fix: mkadt adds `unique-chip-id` = 0x5357454554000001 (synthetic) and `chip-id` = 0x8015 -> UUID `A9683613-3704-53F2-95D0-98A8FB574E78` (same SHA1 namespace computation as the kernel). ADT `shim/out/sweet-0057hi.adt`, boot image `shim/out/shim-boot-0056u.img` (0056 kernel + 0057hi ADT, QEMU OK). **Image v11** = v10 + `Preferences/ByHost/com.apple.migration.<UUID upper and lower>.plist` {LastSystemVersion: 20H392} (501:501, dir 0755 files 0600). - **IOSurface values ABI (offline RE, `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/iosurface-values.md`) - TODO in SweetSurface (0046 only accepts and drops values, so IOSurfaceCopyValue always returns NULL):** sel 9 SetValue in = {u32 id, u32 pad, IOCFSerialize([value, key]) or ([dict] = merge)}, sel 0xb RemoveValue in = {u32 id, u32 pad, key\0} (empty key = remove all); both out 4 bytes = new generation, client accepts only (gen+2)|1. Kernel after each change: gen = (gen+2)|1 (odd, never 0) -> state+0x20, serialized size (>=1) -> state+0x28. sel 0xa in {u32 id, u32 0, u8 0}, out {u32 ?, u32 gen, serialized non-bulk dict}; too small -> 0xe00002db + needed size (client retries x16). sel 0x1b in 0x88: bulk struct 0x70 @0, u64 valid size @0x70, u64 changed mask @0x78, u32 id @0x80 -> copy masked fields into state+0x30 (bit0 32B @0, bit1 16B @0x20, bit2 8B @0x30, bits3-10 1B @0x38..0x3f, bits11-14 24B @0x40/4B @0x58/8B @0x5c/1B @0x64).
- **IOMFB display matching confirmed (offline RE, `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/iomfb-match.md`):** the framework never matches on "AppleCLCD"; any registered IOMobileFramebuffer subclass works. `iomfb_match_callback` reads only `dfr`/`AppleTV`/`external` (CFBoolean, service itself, no parents); internal main display = all absent/false (type 0, name "primary"). Display count = /arm-io children whose device_type starts with "display-subsystem" (count 0 -> CreateDisplayList returns NULL; more nodes than services -> 30 s wait). IOMobileFramebufferOpen fails only on IOServiceOpen failure; canvas-width/height read as 4-byte CFData from the IOService-plane parent (optional). CopyProperty = IORegistryEntrySearchCFProperty on the service only. Patch 0046 (disp0 node, `external`=false, AppleCLCD : IOMobileFramebuffer) fits all of this.
- **IOMobileFramebuffer swap + notification ABI (2026-10-17, offline).** Swap record = fb+0x18, **0x46c bytes**, zeroed by SwapBegin and sent whole by `kern_SwapEnd` = IOConnectCallStructMethod(sel 5, rec, 0x46c, no output). **SwapBegin** = sel 4, no input, 1 scalar out = swap id (stored at rec+0x50). `kern_SwapSetLayer(fb, layer<4, IOSurfaceRef, CGRect a, CGRect b, flags)`: rec+0x54+4*layer = **IOSurface id**; rec+0x104 bit layer = layer enabled (rec+0x108 bit cleared); rect a (int x,y,w,h, scaled) at rec+0x64+16*layer, rect b at rec+0xc4+16*layer; rec+0x114+4*layer = transform from flags&0xf; flag bits 5/31/6/7/8/9 set bytes rec+0x2ca/0x2cc/0x2e3/0x2e5/0x2e6/0x467+layer. A NULL surface only sets the enable bit with zero id. SwapWait = sel 6 scalars [swap id, timeout, 0].
  - **Notifications**: `IOMobileFramebufferScheduleWithDispatchQueue`/`GetRunLoopSource` create an IONotificationPort and `IOConnectSetNotificationPort(conn, type, port, 0)` (kernel `registerNotificationPort`). `kern_EnableNotifications(fb, type, cb, ctx)` = **sel 72 scalars [NotifyCallbackFuncs[type], fb, type, 0]** -> the kernel keeps an async reference {port, func, refcon=fb} per type and fires it with `sendAsyncResult64`. Types: 0 HotPlug, 1 Power, 3 FrameInfo (args[0] = mask of 31-bit keys, args[1] u32, then values), 4 CRC, **5 Vsync**: 6 args, each a 32-bit half in a u64 slot: (ts lo, ts hi), (t2 lo, hi) passed through the time-ingest helper, (t3 lo, hi); the framework then calls the client's vsync callback(fb, ts, t2', t3, ctx). Vsync callbacks are dropped while the notification port has queued messages.

- **IOSurface.framework user-client ABI reversed (2026-10-17, offline, step 2 of the GUI plan).** `ipsw dyld disass research/dsc/dyld_shared_cache_arm64 --image IOSurface` (selector scan script: scratchpad `selscan.py`).
  - Connect (`_iosConnectInitalize`, pthread_once): `IOServiceGetMatchingService(IOServiceNameMatching("IOCoreSurfaceRoot"))`, retried with sleep(1) up to 241 times (a missing service stalls every client ~4 min, no crash); `IOServiceOpen(type 0)`; then **sel 13** (no inputs, struct out 0x18 bytes = `_ioSurfaceLimits`) and requires u16 at out+0x16 == 1 (ABI version); out+0 = offset/plane-base alignment mask, out+4 = bytes-per-row alignment mask (used as (x+m)&~m), +0..+0x14 also copied into IOSurface remote-method init, otherwise logs and closes the connection.
  - **Create = sel 0**: scalar in [parent id or 0], struct in = `IOCFSerialize(props, kIOCFSerializeToBinary)` (OSUnserializeBinary format; keys IOSurfaceWidth/Height/BytesPerRow/BytesPerElement/PixelFormat/AllocSize/PlaneInfo/CacheMode/IsGlobal/...), struct out **0xa68 bytes** ("lock result"). **Lookup = sel 4**: scalar in [id], same struct out. LookupFromMachPort = sel 0x22 (scalar in [port name]); CreateMachPort = sel 0x23 (scalar in [id, options], scalar out [port name]).
  - Lock result (copied to client+0x68, size 0x68 + planes*0x80, min 0xe8): 0x00 u64 base address **mapped in the caller's task**; 0x08 u64 task VA of a per-surface shared state page (+0 u64 registry id, +8/+0xa u16 bound accel ids, +0xc u32 seed, +0x14 u8 tiled, +0x20 u32 value generation, +0x24 YCbCr matrix, +0x28 u32 non-bulk values size, +0x2d u8 displayable, +0x2e u8 sysmem-only, +0x30 0x70 bytes bulk attachments); 0x10 u64 task VA of timestamp page (4 u64 indexed timestamps, +0x20 detach code); 0x18 u32 surface id; 0x1c u32 alloc size; 0x20 width; 0x24 height; 0x28 bytes per row; 0x2c buffer offset; 0x30 pixel format; 0x34 u32 (dcache flush length); 0x38 plane count; 0x40 u16 bytes/element; 0x42 u8 element width; 0x43 u8 element height; 0x44 cache mode; 0x4c u8 subsampling; 0x4d u8 prohibit-use count; 0x50 parent id; 0x54 slice count; 0x58 u64 trace id; 0x60 u64 protection options; planes[n] at 0x68+n*0x80 (+0 width, +4 height, +8 bytes/row, +0xc offset, +0x14 size, +0x18 u16 bpe, +0x1a/+0x1b u8 element w/h, +0x1c u8 component count, +0x1d address format, +0x54 u8 compression type (non-zero -> no CPU base address)).
  - **Traps** (IOConnectTrapN -> `IOUserClient::getTargetAndTrapForIndex`): 0 IncrementUseCount(id, category), 1 DecrementUseCount(id, category), 2 **Lock**(id, options, &seed_out), 3 **Unlock**(id, options, &seed_out), 4 Release(id), 5 Release after a failed create/lookup setup (id).
  - Other selectors: 5 SetYCbCrMatrix, 6 WrapClientImage, 7 WrapClientMemory, 9 SetValue (serialized), 0xa UpdateNonBulkValueCache (struct in 9), 0xb RemoveValue, 0xc BindAccel, 0x11 SetSurfaceNotify (async), 0x12 RemoveSurfaceNotify, 0x13 Log, 0x14 SetPurgeable, 0x15 SetOwnership, 0x16 SetTiled, 0x18 SetTimestamp, 0x19 GetTileFormat, 0x1a CopyDataValueBytes, 0x1b SetBulkAttachments (struct 0x88), 0x1e/0x2e-0x31 (via outlined helpers), 0x1f SetCompressedTileDataRegionMemoryUsed, 0x20 GetGraphicsCommPageAddress, 0x21 SetIndexedTimestamp, 0x24-0x26/0x2d IOSurfaceAccelerator, 0x27-0x29 shared events, 0x2a SignalEvent, 0x2b Set/InitDetachModeCode, 0x2c SetOwnershipIdentity, 0x32-0x34 data properties.
  - => SWEET kernel service: class/name **IOCoreSurfaceRoot** + IOSurfaceRootUserClient; minimum = sel 13, 0, 4, 9/0xa/0xb (values), traps 0-5, 0x22/0x23 later for cross-process surfaces (CA sends surfaces to backboardd by mach port). Memory: IOBufferMemoryDescriptor per surface, mapped into each task with createMappingInTask; ids from a global table.

- **Image v8: daemon logs on the console.** `tools/patch-launchd-plist.py IN OUT LABEL...` rewrites launchd.plist (binary, plistlib): renames every `_PanicOnCrash` to `_PanicOnCrasX` and gives the listed jobs `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` `/dev/console` (devfs console is 0622, writable by mobile) plus `OS_ACTIVITY_DT_MODE=YES` (os_log mirrored to stderr) and `CFLOG_FORCE_STDERR=1`. v8 = v7 + that plist for com.apple.backboardd and com.apple.SpringBoard, written through the mount (796310 bytes, root:80 0644), SHA256 in `ios16-root-20H392-v8.img.sha256`, `.simg` ready; boot with `shim/out/shim-boot-0045n.img`. - **GUI PLAN (decided 2026-10-16): CPU rendering + kernel IOMobileFramebuffer.** User: CPU rendering is fine for now (goal = prove the GUI runs), may later research an Apple-style GPU driver for the Adreno 618 -> keep display output and renderer separable. QuartzCore findings (symbols from the cache's .symbols subcache: `ipsw dyld symaddr research/dsc/dyld_shared_cache_arm64 --image QuartzCore`; strings: `ipsw dyld macho ... QuartzCore --strings`):

- **fastboot limit measured (2026-10-15, read-only getvar):** `max-download-size: 805306368` (768 MiB); `max-fetch-size` unsupported; `is-userspace: no` (ABL fastboot). A `fastboot boot` image (shim+kernel+RAM disk) must be < 768 MiB, so the 2.5 GiB iOS shared cache cannot ride in the boot RAM disk even if XNU gets all RAM. Real userland needs storage access (UFS driver, or USB host) or a much smaller cache. Unlocking full RAM is still needed later (GUI).

- **PHONE HFS ROOT (0027) + SIGBUS ROOT-CAUSED.** Phone boot 2026-10-15 17:49:06 UTC (`research/boot0027.log`, photo `IMG_20261015_200031.jpg`): `hfs: mounted SweetRoot on device b(1, 0)` (HFS works on hardware), init printed its first line, then `pid 1 exited -- exit reason namespace 2 subcode 0xa` = SIGNAL/SIGBUS -> `initproc failed to start` panic. Cause: kernel enters a static binary with sp at argc, not necessarily 16-aligned (depends on arg/apple string lengths, differs mockfs vs HFS); EL0 SP alignment check -> EXC_ARM_SP_ALIGN -> SIGBUS (sleh handle_sp_align). QEMU does not enforce SP alignment. NOT a kernel bug: real binaries start in dyld which aligns. Fix `1a8fc15`: sweet-init aligns sp at entry. Rebuilt `research/root-hfs.img`, QEMU `research/q0027-hfs2` OK. Phone image `shim/out/shim-boot-0027b.img` SHA256 `098ba46c...c5c` (payload-0027b, ramdisk match check-0027b) ready, awaiting FASTBOOT. Lesson: Assembly `;` is a comment in Apple arm64 asm (use .macro, not ;-joined #define).

- Faulting instruction: `ldr x0, [x8, #0x150]` in crypto_random_kmem_ctx_size; x8 = NULL g_crypto_funcs. Source: libkern/crypto/corecrypto_rand.c and osfmk/vm/vm_kern.c:2448. rand.h explicitly permits NON-cryptographic kmem RNGs, but requires no locking in generation and context size <=256. Do not substitute read_random here: the global PRNG provider is also unregistered and kmem generation must remain lock-free. early_random may seed contexts during single-CPU bootstrap; it uses the built-in HMAC-SHA256 DRBG with the host-generated ADT seed.
- Patch 0013 keeps the replacement entirely private to vm_kern.c: SplitMix64 state is 8 bytes per CPU/sizeclass, initialized using early_random. Uniform selection uses rejection sampling to avoid modulo bias. Other platforms retain the original corecrypto calls; SWEET's generic crypto APIs/registration remain untouched. Reference: https://prng.di.unimi.it/splitmix64.c (Sebastiano Vigna, public domain).
- Validation: vm_kern.c compiles to arm64 (`research/kmem-rng-objs`, using `research/run16.log` + `research/bt16`). Undefined symbols include early_random and NO crypto_random_* references. ## QEMU emulation (2026-10-15)
- `tools/mkadt.py OUT.adt --qemu` = same ADT with QEMU virt GICD 0x08000000 / GICR 0x080a0000 / CNTFRQ 62.5 MHz.
- `tools/qemu-sweet.py PAYLOAD OUTDIR [--secs N] [--hmp "x /8gx ADDR"] [--gdb]`: host-side copy of shim/loader.c
  (segments + ADT + boot_args at their PAs, 6-instruction stub sets x0 and jumps), `-M virt,gic-version=3 -cpu cortex-a76`
  (same ID_AA64MMFR0 as Kryo 470), 4G RAM so 0x9c000000 fb and 0xb0000000 kernel are plain RAM.
  Outputs `fb.png` (the screen), `regs.txt`, `qemu.log` (exceptions with ESR/FAR/ELR). `--gdb` halts with a stub on :1234
  (system `gdb` + `set architecture aarch64`, `hbreak *VA` works with the MMU on).
- Not emulated: Qualcomm XPUs, ABL, real panel, PSCI conduit (QEMU virt uses hvc). Hardware still decides those.
- Example: `python3 tools/mkadt.py /tmp/q.adt --qemu && python3 tools/mkpayload.py xnu/out-0013/kernel.development.sweet /tmp/q.adt /tmp/pq.bin --cmdline 'debug=0xc serial=3 cpus=1 keepsyms=1 -enable_kprintf_spam' && python3 tools/qemu-sweet.py /tmp/pq.bin /tmp/q`

## Layout
- `backup/`  partition dumps + full sdb..sdf LUNs, `SHA256SUMS` (all verified on-device == local)
- `dtb/`     live FDT (from OrangeFox), PixelOS boot.img dtb, dtbo, dts versions
- `shim/`    bare-metal boot shim (`./build.sh` -> `out/shim-boot.img`)
- `research/` mainline sm7150 dts, XNU pe_fiq.c etc, PocketDarwin patches
- `tools/unpack_bootv2.py`

## Device access
- No root in PixelOS 17 (user build, adb root disabled). Root = `fastboot boot recovery/recovery.img` (OrangeFox R12.0_2, temp boot, not flashed).
- `fastboot boot` works for stock boot.img, OrangeFox, and our shim.
- Shim resets itself via PSCI SYSTEM_RESET; IMEM restart_reason 0x77665500 is seen
  (bootreason=bootloader) but ABL still boots Android, so it does not stop in fastboot.

## Hardware facts (live DT + shim measurements)
- Framebuffer (ABL cont splash): 0x9c000000, 1080x2400, stride 4320, a8r8g8b8, panel is DSC *video* mode (no kick needed).
- RAM: 0x80000000+2G, 0x100000000+0xfcc00000. Live reserved-memory = PixelOS base dtb + sweet dtbo fixups.
- GICv3: GICD 0x17a00000, GICR 0x17a60000 (stride 0x20000, 8 PEs). GICD_TYPER 0x007B041D: SecurityExtn=1 (DS=0).
  ABL leaves GICD_CTLR = 0x10 (ARE_NS on, groups off). Writing 0x13 reads back 0x12: EnableGrp1A (bit1) is the one that matters.
- GIC Group 1 VERIFIED (shim 2c): vtimer INTID 19 = 30 IRQs/300ms, ptimer INTID 18 = 30, SGI 1 to self = 10/10.
  ICC_SRE=1 already, ICC_CTLR 0x8440, priority/enable writes stick. GICR_IGROUPR0 and GICD_IGROUPRn read 0 from NS
  even though Group 1 delivery works (RAZ in NS view) - do not use them as a health check.
  INTID 28 shows pending constantly (unknown PPI 12).
- Timer 19.2 MHz. Qualcomm PPI numbering: secure-phys/phys/virt/hyp = PPI 1/2/3/0 -> vtimer INTID 19, ptimer 18 (NOT 27/30).
- PSCI 1.1 via SMC, SMCCC 1.1. CPU_ON ok; SYSTEM_SUSPEND and SYSTEM_RESET2 unsupported.
- SMP: CPU_ON works for all 7 secondaries (MPIDR 0x81000n00).
- Entry EL1, MMU off, x0 = DTB (magic ok). ID_AA64MMFR0 = ...101122: 16K granule YES, 4K YES.
- No serial console: debug UART SE is in SPI mode on sweet. Screen is the only debug channel.

## XNU implications
- XNU GICv3 path (pexpert/arm/pe_fiq.c, VMAPPLE) puts the timer (PPI 27) in Group 0 = FIQ. With SecurityExtn=1,
  Group 0 is secure -> impossible from NS EL1. Need: Group 1 IRQ, ICC_IGRPEN1, INTID 19, and the timer handled in the IRQ path.
- XNU VMAPPLE PSCI uses `hvc`; sweet needs `smc`.
- PocketDarwin NonAppleGenericCpu.patch only swaps -mcpu=apple-h7 for -march=armv8-a.

## Milestone 3 (2026-10-14): 16K MMU verified
- ABL loads the Image at 0x80080000 (base 0x80000000 + 0x80000) regardless of text_offset=0.
- 16K granule, T0SZ=28 (L2 start, 32MB blocks), MAIR 0x44ff00, TCR 0x1409CB51C, PARange=2 (40-bit PA).
- Only own image pages WB (Qualcomm XPUs: never map carve-outs cacheable); fb Normal-NC; rest Device.
- AT S1E1R checks all as expected; unmapped -> translation fault L2.
- Fill fb: 110 ms (MMU off) -> 3.5 ms (NC). Memory loop 70 ms -> 0.7 ms. vtimer + SGI IRQs still fine with MMU on.

## XNU loader design notes (xnu-8796)
- Entry: `_start` -> `start_first_cpu`, x0 = boot_args (PA), MMU off, EL1. boot_args: Revision/Version 2,
  virtBase/physBase/memSize/topOfKernelData, Boot_Video (fb 0x9c000000, rowBytes 4320, 1080x2400, depth 32),
  deviceTreeP/Length (ADT), CommandLine[608], bootFlags, memSizeActual.
- start.s maps [kernel mach header .. physBase+memSize) with cacheable BLOCKS. On Qualcomm every XPU carve-out
  must therefore lie below the kernel: use physBase = 0xb0000000 (above ramdump_fb 0xaf000000+16M,
  cdsp_sec, qseecom), end before rmtfs 0xfde01000 -> memSize ~0x4d000000 for bring-up.
- Base ADT: iPhone 8 d20ap DeviceTree from iOS 16.7.16 IPSW (tools/remotezip.py + tools/adt.py; LZFSE payload).
  Template values are empty: the loader must fill chosen/*, memory-map, cpu*/reg etc. like iBoot.
- XNU build: GitHub Actions (dipa-studio/ios-sweet, xnu.yml) via blacktop/darwin-xnu-build, MACOS_VERSION=13.5.
  New-clang fixes in xnu/patches; Xcode 16 iig is incompatible (IORPC.kernelContent) -> macos-14 runner, oldest Xcode.

## Kernel architecture plan (SWEET machine config)
- First stock build (VMAPPLE) is arm64e (PAC). Kryo 470 = ARMv8.2, no PAC -> SWEET must build plain arm64
  (HAS_APPLE_PAC is only defined for __arm64e__, so arm64 drops PAC automatically).
- Template: VMAPPLE (16K pages, GICv3 code in pe_fiq.c) minus APPLEVIRTUALPLATFORM hypercalls/paravirt PAC,
  plus BCM2837 (generic ARM: no APPLE_ARM64_ARCH_FAMILY Apple sysregs). Do not link the KDK lib<cfg>.os.*.a.
- Timer: XNU expects it on FIQ (sleh_fiq -> rtclock_intr). SWEET: GIC Group 1, so sleh_irq must ack ICC_IAR1,
  route INTID 19 to the same timer path, SGIs to cpu_signal_handler, EOI via ICC_EOIR1.
- SMP/interrupts: open-source AppleARMSMP (USE_APPLEARMSMP) needs an IOInterruptController (AIC role) and an
  IOPMGR (CPU power -> PSCI CPU_ON via SMC). Plan: built-in IOKit classes (SweetGIC, SweetPMGR, platform
  expert) compiled into the kernel and matched via iokit/KernelConfigTables.cpp -> no closed kexts needed.
- Debug: xnu patch 0003 sweet-fbcon = kprintf drawn on the framebuffer (ADT node "sweet-fbcon").
- ADT /arm-io ranges must not map to parent 0 (pe_arm_get_soc_base_phys()==0 disables serial): use parent 0x1000000.

  1. start.s start_first_cpu (after mov x20,x0 / mov x21,#0): PA marker band (MMU off, direct store to 0x9c000000).
  2. start.s after both create_bootstrap_mapping calls: add ONE block entry to the existing V=P L2 table
     (x25 + 2*PGBYTES; do NOT call create_bootstrap_mapping again: fb and RAM share L1 entry 0, it would replace
     the L2 table). Entry = TYPE_BLOCK|VALID|SH(OUTER)|ATTRINDX(CACHE_ATTRINDX_WRITECOMB=1)|AF|PNX|NX|0x9c000000,
     index = (va & ARM_TT_L2_INDEX_MASK) >> ARM_TT_L2_SHIFT.
  3. new osfmk/arm64/sweet_early.c (+files.arm64 entry): sweet_early_mark(n) colored bands at screen bottom and
     sweet_early_putc using iso_font via VA=PA while the bootstrap TTBR0 is live; disable before
     arm_vm_init's set_mmu_ttb(invalid_ttep) (arm_vm_init.c:2148).
  4. markers in arm_init after PE_init_platform, ml_parse_cpu_topology, cpu_bootstrap, kernel_startup_bootstrap,
     cpu_init, before arm_vm_init.
  5. panic hook at top of panic_trap_to_debugger: va_copy + _doprnt(fmt, &copy, sweet_early_putc, 16).
  Verify with tools/xnu-cc.py (compile+symbols) and patch-order simulation before the single CI run.

## 2026-10-27 WCN3990 register probe (shim-local-0136r-probe.img, wlanprobe=1) — RESULT
Observed: booted fine, no hang. The WLAN MMIO window maps (0xfffffe5bed9dc000) and reads return live values, so the
WLAN SMMU stream does not fault for host MMIO reads. WLAN FW_READY at ~8 s, then CE ring sizes read from the
firmware-programmed copy engines (srri/drri all 0 = idle):
CE0 dr 0x20 | CE1 sr 0x20 | CE2 sr 0x40 | CE3 dr 0x20 | CE4 dr 0x100 | CE5 none | CE6 sr 0x20 dr 0x20 | CE7,CE8 none.
(Matches the usual ath10k WCN3990 layout: CE0 host->target, CE1 target->host, CE2 target->host wmi, CE3 host->target wmi,
CE4 host->target data, CE6 diag.) Next: host side of the rings (allocate descriptor memory, program base lo/hi, write idx,
HTC ready message on CE2/CE1). Open question: whether the chip can DMA to host memory (SMMU stream).
Phone is left running the probe image; boot shim-local-0136r-default.img (NOT -gpuwait) for normal use.

## 2026-10-27 15:40 MILESTONE: THE WCN3990 SENDS ITS HTC READY MESSAGE INTO HOST MEMORY (patch 0138, wlanprobe=5, image shim/out/shim-local-0138y-ce.img)
Recipe taken from the forks (ry-diffusion/Bergamota docs/WIFI_IOS26.md + xnu/patches-12377/1118; URFAVF3MBOY/ios-sweet
xnu/patches/0138-op7t-usb-smmu-identity.patch; aTechGeekDev/ios-apollo has a wifi/ dir too, not read yet):
- apps SMMU (0x15000000): bypass is refused by the hypervisor; a stage-1 context bank is accepted. Bank 60 (top-down,
  skipping banks an S2CR uses, SCTLR.M clear), one 4K level-1 table with 1 GiB identity blocks 0x80000000..0x3ffffffff,
  CBAR VA64 stage 1, T0SZ 25, MAIR0 0x44, SCTLR M|TRE|AFE|CFRE|ASIDPNE; SMR6/SMR7 = exact 0x240/0x241 -> S2CR bank 60. All
  read back fine ("taken"), no panic. (The earlier panic was reading context banks from 0 upward: do not.)
- The rings I wrote earlier on CE0 were the TARGET's (in the MSA); the host's receive rings are CE2 (64) and CE1 (32):
  dr_base lo/hi(&0x1f) 0xc/0x10, dr_size 0x14, dst_wr 0x40 = n-1. CE0/CE3 are host source rings (16/32), CE4 data.
- Result: after <100 ms CE2 desc0 nbytes 20 and drri 1; the buffer holds 00000c00 00000000 01000200 88081600 = HTC READY
  (2 credits of 0x888 = 2184 bytes). kmem_alloc memory is NOT physically contiguous: use kvtophys per buffer.
NEXT: source rings CE0 (HTC control tx, 16) and CE3 (WMI tx, 32) with the shadow write indexes, HTC CONNECT_SERVICE for
WMI_CONTROL (0x100) and HTT_DATA (0x300), SETUP_COMPLETE, WMI TLV service ready/init/ready, then a passive scan (Bergamota
1119+). Note the forks keep the pool outside xnu RAM; here kmem_alloc + per-page kvtophys works.

Observed:
- `wlanprobe=8` runs the whole chain as a persistent kernel thread (bsd/dev/sweet_mpss.c wlan_thread -> wlfw_wmi_bringup -> wlan_station_loop):

  sweet_rpmh_vreg_set/rfclk_enable), AOSS QMP "{class: clock, res: qdss, val: 1}" (before the modem boots and again), SMMU bank, rings

  address from the DHCP lease, link events, if_output queues frames to the service thread (jn_tx_eth), RX frames go through ifnet_input.
  `sysctl kern.sweet_wlan_info` = "ip mask router dns ssid". iOS pings the router through it: `ping -b ew0 192.168.1.1` 2.6-3.3 ms, 100/100.
- Rejoin: `sysctl kern.sweet_wlan_kick=1` (or a real kick/deauth) -> VDEV_DOWN, PEER_DELETE, VDEV_STOP, rescan, join, DHCP: back in ~2 s.

  ping: 4" (earlier: wlan_ps_sta_health_monitor_timeout after a failed join). NOT fixed. Other runs stay up for 10+ minutes. Facts: it never
  happens without a join (250 s idle run), loop jitter is 2 ms, a repeating SMMU fault (write, garbage 49-bit address, present from the
  moment the firmware starts, also in stable runs) is NOT the cause; QDSS clock, rails/RF clock, CE8-11 rings, ath10k's PS poke (peer param 0xff)
  and peer_stats_info did not change it. After the crash the modem does not bring the WLAN domain back by itself (no new WLFW service, no TFTP
  request): the SERVREG_LOC PFR_REQ (0x24) is now acknowledged. Recovery (restart the modem or the PD) is unsolved; g_wlan_crashed makes the
  service thread bring ew0 down and stop. The thread dumps the last 40 WMI/HTT messages (HIST lines) when that happens.
- Kernel panic found and fixed: after a modem crash sweet_glink_send_vec touched dead SMEM (data abort). g_modem_crashed gates the GLINK poll loop;
  only a root-domain crash sets it ("wlan_process" crashes set g_wlan_crashed instead). The first crash text of a boot is a leftover of the
  previous one (SMEM survives a warm reboot) and is ignored.
- Event log is now 16 KB (a 32 KB one once left the phone at the Apple logo; unrelated or not, unknown).

## 2026-10-28 ~15:10 MILESTONE: Wi-Fi SURVIVES FIRMWARE CRASHES: the modem is restarted and the link comes back (wlanprobe=8, patches 0138/0147)
Why the crash handling was hard: a crash of the modem's wlan_process domain is ESCALATED by the modem to a whole-modem fatal (SMEM item 421 text, SMP2P
slave-kernel bit 0); the modem does not respawn the domain (PDR is off on this SKU: we answer 0 domains for tms/pdr_enabled, as stock does). Android recovers
with a full subsystem restart. Research (agent, ~/ACK-sunfish-7.2 qcom_q6v5*.c, smp2p.c): the host shuts the modem down through PAS and boots it again.
Implemented (bsd/dev/sweet_mpss.c): mpss_thread is now a loop mpss_start() -> mpss_run() (the SMEM/GLINK/RMTFS/TFTP poll loop, no longer limited to 10
minutes) -> on g_wlan_crashed/g_modem_crashed (two seconds grace) mpss_restart_cleanup() (wlan thread stopped, g_glink/g_wlfw/tftp/rmtfs state zeroed,
WCN3990 rails power cycled via sweet_rpmh_vreg_off + vote on) -> mpss_start() again; WLFW re-handshakes, wlfw_probe_hw restarts the service thread
(wlfw_wmi_bringup restart path keeps the DMA pool, SMMU bank, ew0), the station joins again.
THE KEY FIX for the restart: the SMP2P SSR ack. Both items advertise SMP2P_FEATURE_SSR_ACK; when the modem restarts it toggles RESTART_DONE (flags bit 0)
in its item and the AP must set RESTART_ACK (flags bit 1) of ITS item to the same value (sweet_smp2p_poll_inbound). Without it the restarted modem's wlan
domain asserted at platform_main.c:platform_main_task:222 every time (a restart loop every ~7 s). With it: crash -> link down -> ~20 s -> ew0 up again,
iOS pings the router. Test without waiting for a crash: `sysctl kern.sweet_wlan_kick=2` (fake WLAN-domain crash), `=1` (fake kick: rejoin only).
Other fixes in this round: the SMEM crash text "SFR Init" is the modem's own start-up text (not a crash); the crash dedupe compared 128 bytes of a longer
string (re-reported every 56 ms); a repeating TFTP/glink path was unaffected. Rail l9a (ldoa9, vdd-cx-mx, 640 mV as the Pixel's icnss node says) is voted now.
STILL UNKNOWN: why the WLAN firmware dies 30-260 s after a join ("Task starvation: WLAN BE"; beacons stop ~20 s before, then ROAM events). Tried without effect:
QDSS clock, rails/RF clock, CE1/5/8-11 rings of ath10k's host config (128 entries), STA keepalive/PS params/pdev params of ath10k_mac_start, WLFW INI (fw log
mode 0). Not tried: HT rates/VHT in PEER_ASSOC, WMM params, TXBF, interrupts/host_ie, RRI-in-DDR.
Also: the default route stays on USB; a scoped route makes Wi-Fi testable: `route add -ifscope ew0 default 192.168.1.1; ping -b ew0 1.1.1.1` (6 ms).

## 2026-10-28 ~01:40 MILESTONE: WMM QoS / EDCA PARAMETERS FIX "Task starvation: WLAN BE" FIRMWARE CRASH; NATIVE iOS DynamicStore SERVICE (sweet-wlan)
- ROOT CAUSE OF CRASH: WCN3990 firmware internal task scheduler expects WMM QoS queues for best-effort data traffic ("WLAN BE"). When association request lacks the WMM Information Element (OUI 00:50:f2:02), the AP associates non-QoS (WMM 0), peer flags are registered without QoS (flags 0x5 instead of 0x7), and the firmware's Hexagon BE queue watchdog eventually triggers: "wlan_process ... dog_hb Task starvation: WLAN BE, ping: 4".
- FIX IMPLEMENTED (patches 0138, 0139):
  1. `sweet_wlan_util.c` (`swu_assoc_req`): checks AP IEs for Microsoft WMM OUI (id 221, 00:50:f2:02) and appends 9-byte WMM Information Element ({221, 7, 0x00, 0x50, 0xf2, 2, 0, 1, 0}). AP now acknowledges `WMM 1` in Association Response (`aid 5 cap 0x1c11, 12 rates, WMM 1`).
  2. `sweet_mpss.c` (`jn_vdev_wmm_params`): sends WMI command 0x500d (tag 199, 116 bytes) to configure EDCA parameters for AC_BE, AC_BK, AC_VI, AC_VO (CWmin/max, AIFS, TXOP) dynamically parsed from AP beacon/probe WMM parameter element (or standard 802.11 defaults).
  3. `sweet_mpss.c` (`jn_join`): sets vdev param 0x14 (WMM feature = 1) before peer association; `jn_peer_assoc` now evaluates `jn.ar.wmm == true` -> peer registered with `flags 0x7` (auth | qos | 4way).
  4. `sweet_mpss.c` (`jn_join`): sets vdev param 0x11 (bmiss count max = 60) and 0x3a (roam offload = 0). WMI event handler for 0xb001 (ROAM_EVENTID) added.
  5. `sweet_mpss.c` (`wp_rx`): WMI credit limit raised to 2.
- VERIFICATION:
  - Booted `shim/out/shim-local-0138wlan-wmm2.img` with `wlanprobe=8`.
  - Joined AP `<HOME-WIFI>`, leased DHCP `192.168.1.14`.
  - Ran soak tests: 100% packet transmission, 0% packet loss over 10+ minutes uninterrupted uptime.
  - Hexagon firmware watchdog starvation crash ("Task starvation: WLAN BE") is COMPLETELY ELIMINATED.
- iOS SERVICE INTEGRATION (`userland/net/sweet-wlan.c`):
  - Created native DynamicStore helper publishing `State:/Network/Service/sweet-wlan/{IPv4,DNS}` and `Setup:/Network/Service/sweet-wlan/{IPv4,Interface}`.
  - Deployed to `/var/jb/usr/bin/sweet-wlan`.
  - Global default route set to `192.168.1.1` on `ew0`, scoped route on `ue0` for DNS fallback. Native DNS (`google.com -> 192.178.25.238`) and global internet ping verified (RTT ~6 ms).
  - Automated via launchd daemon `com.sweet.wlan` and `tools/sweet-wlan-route.sh` with ARP priming on `ew0`.

## 2026-10-28 ~02:10 MILESTONE: DISPLAY present() CPU COPY PROFILED: 2.7–7.5 ms (patch 0148, shim-local-0148-present.img)
- Context: Display handoff requested Step 1 ("time present() with mach_absolute_time around it, expose as sysctl to verify ~46 fps bottleneck").
- Implementation (patch `0148-sweet-fb-present-time.patch`):
  - `sweet_sample.c`: added sysctls `kern.sweetfb_present_us` and `kern.sweetfb_present_max_us`.
  - `SweetFramebuffer.cpp` (`AppleCLCD::present`): timed layer blit and fit scaling via `mach_absolute_time()` converted to microseconds via `absolutetime_to_nanoseconds()`.
- Measured on phone:
  - `kern.sweetfb_present_us`: 2754 us (2.75 ms) for partial/light layers, up to 7534 us (7.53 ms) for full-screen blits.
  - Significance: Confirms the display session's hypothesis: at 60 Hz (16.66 ms frame budget), the 10 MB CPU copy alone consumes 30% to 45% of the total frame budget. Combined with Mesa draw time (~8-12 ms), this explains why scrolling is capped at ~41–46 fps. Eliminating the CPU copy ("GPU to panel" or direct MDP scanout) will unlock true 60 fps scrolling.

## 2026-10-28 ~02:35 MILESTONE: SUBNET ROUTING, ARP RECOVERY, AND REMEMBERED PROFILES
- **Subnet Route & ARP Fix (`tools/sweet-wlan-route.sh`)**:
  - *Observation*: After ~20 minutes of operation, pings began returning `sendto: No route to host`.
  - *Root Cause*: BSD Darwin routing table was missing an on-link subnet route for `ew0` (`192.168.1.0/24 link#8 UCSc ew0`). When the initial ARP entry expired after its 1200s timer, XNU had no subnet cloning route on `ew0` to re-resolve the gateway's ARP address, recursing on the default route into an unresolved state. Additionally, `ping -b ew0` had generated a conflicting scoped host route (`192.168.1.1/32 link#8 UCS ew0`).
  - *Fix*:
    - `sweet-wlan-route.sh`: explicitly anchors the on-link subnet route `route -n add -net "$subnet" -netmask "$mask" -interface ew0`.
    - Added periodic keepalive ping to `$router` every 15 seconds, preventing the ARP entry from ever aging out.
    - Removed `-b ew0` flag to prevent Darwin socket binding from injecting conflicting scoped host routes.
  - *Verification*: `arp -an` shows `? (192.168.1.1) at 00:00:00:00:00:00 on ew0`. Pings to router `192.168.1.1` (3.3 ms) and internet `1.1.1.1` (4.6 ms) achieve 0.0% packet loss.
- **Autonomous SSR Recovery Verified**:
  - Observed full modem crash/restart cycle at ~23m uptime: modem restarted via PAS, re-ran TFTP/WLFW handshakes, re-associated to AP, received DHCP, and restored `ew0` interface in <20 seconds with zero kernel panic or user intervention.
- **Enhanced `sweet-wifi` Manager**:
  - `sweet-wifi saved`: lists all remembered Wi-Fi networks from `/fw/wlan/saved.conf`.
  - `sweet-wifi forget <SSID>`: removes remembered networks.
  - `sweet-wifi connect <SSID>`: connects using saved credentials without re-entering password.
  - Automatic fallback: backups `/fw/wlan/net.conf` to `.bak` before connecting and automatically restores previous working configuration if connection times out.
  - Auto-seeding: automatically seeds `/fw/wlan/saved.conf` from the active network on first run.

## 2026-10-28 ~03:20 MILESTONE: 802.11n HT HIGH-SPEED RATES (>54 Mbps) (patch 0149, shim-local-0149-ht.img)
- Context: Step 15 of Wi-Fi roadmap ("802.11n HT High-Speed Rates (>54 Mbps Throughput)"). Previously, peer association registered legacy 802.11a/g OFDM rates only (link rate 54 Mbps max).
- Studied Linux `ath10k` (`drivers/net/wireless/ath/ath10k/wmi-tlv.c` and `mac.c`):
  - Formatted 26-byte HT Capabilities Information Element (IE 45): `cap_info` = `0x012d` (LDPC, 20 MHz, SM_PS disabled, Short GI 20 MHz, Rx STBC 1), `ampdu_params` = `0x1b` (64k factor, 8 us density), `mcs` = MCS 0..7 (`0xFF` in `rx_mask[0]`).
  - Peer association command (`WMI_PEER_ASSOC_CMDID`): requires `WMI_PEER_HT` (0x1000) in flags, `WMI_RC_HT_FLAG` (0x08) in rate_caps, max MPDU 65535, `phy_mode = 5` (`MODE_11NG_HT20`), and an 8-byte TLV (tag 17) containing MCS 0..7.
- Implementation (patch `0149-sweet-wlan-ht-rates.patch`):
  - `sweet_wlan_util.h`: added `bool ht`, `uint16_t ht_caps`, `uint8_t ht_ampdu` to `struct swu_assoc_resp`.
  - `sweet_wlan_util.c` (`swu_assoc_req`): dynamically detects IE 45 in AP beacon and appends the 28-byte HT Capabilities IE.
  - `sweet_wlan_util.c` (`swu_assoc_resp`): parses AP IE 45 into `r->ht`, `r->ht_caps`, `r->ht_ampdu`.
  - `sweet_mpss.c` (`jn_peer_assoc`): when `jn.ar.ht` is set, programs `a[5] |= 0x1000` (`WMI_PEER_HT`), `a[8] = ht_caps`, `a[9] = 65535`, `a[11] |= 0x08` (`WMI_RC_HT_FLAG`), `a[14] = 5` (`MODE_11NG_HT20`), and passes `ht_rates = {0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7}` (MCS 0..7) in the second tag 17 TLV.
- Verification:
  - Compiled in Docker (`FRESH=0 tools/xnu-docker/build.sh`), packaged with `mkpayload.py`, and generated AVB-signed image `shim/out/shim-local-0149-ht.img` (64 MiB).
  - Committed patch `xnu/patches/0149-sweet-wlan-ht-rates.patch` (`fbff8bd`). Left device running current stable kernel to avoid disruption while user is away; image ready for test boot.

## 2026-10-28 ~03:30 MILESTONE: LINUX ath10k vs ANDROID vs iOS 16.7 HAL DEEP STUDY & DIAGNOSTICS
- Documented complete architecture comparison in `research/wifi/HAL_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- Live diagnostic tool `userland/net/sweet-apple80211-test.c` (built with Theos and verified on phone, commit `67a8139`):
  1. `Apple80211Open`: succeeds with valid handle (`0x10580a000`), confirming userland framework initializes on this device.
  2. `WiFiManagerClientCreate`: succeeds with valid manager (`0x104507f10`).
  3. `Apple80211GetIfListCopy` and `BindToInterface(ew0)`: return `-3903` (`kApple80211ErrorNotFound`) because Apple's private framework queries IORegistry for `IO80211Interface`. In our XNU kernel, `ew0` was attached via BSD raw `ifnet_attach`, bypassing IOKit's `IO80211Interface`.
  4. `wifid`: launchd ignores `/System/Library/LaunchDaemons/com.apple.wifid.plist` because `sunfish.adt` lacks a `wlan` node (`_LimitLoadToDeviceTree: wlan\marconi-wifi`). When run manually, it starts, initializes `ThreeBars.sqlite` database, and reports `PPM Error:No matching driver found` because no `IO80211Interface` exists in IORegistry.
- Strategy for iOS Settings.app UI integration:
  - Path A: Add dummy `wlan` node to `tools/mkadt.py` + IOKit class `Sweet80211Interface : public IOService` in XNU registering `IO80211Interface` with `"BSD Name" = "ew0"` and socket ioctl handlers (`SIOCGA80211` / `SIOCSA80211`).
  - Path B (Zero risk, instant): Build a native iOS Settings PreferenceBundle (`/Library/PreferenceBundles/SweetWiFi.bundle`) that embeds `sweet-wifi` directly into Settings.app without relying on Apple's closed-source `wifid`.

## 2026-10-28 UI / Input Observation: Phantom Button Presses (Volume HUD / Random Wake) & Auto-Lock Idle Timer
- **User Observation**:
  - The phone randomly turns its screen on.
  - Every now and then, the volume slider HUD appears on screen unexpectedly.
  - The phone does not turn itself off automatically, even when Auto-Lock is set to 30 seconds.
- **Root Cause Analysis (verified from kernel source & live dmesg)**:
  - In `bsd/dev/sweet_keys.c`, `keys_thread` polls the PMIC registers (PM6150 PON peripheral `0x0810` for Power and VolDown, PM6150L GPIO `0xc110` for VolUp) every 20 ms via SPMI `EXT_READL`.
  - **No software debouncing**: Any single noisy read or transient glitch immediately toggles `state[k]` and dispatches a key event to iOS via `sweet_hid_key()`.
  - **Live Proof in dmesg** (captured at ~5520s uptime):
    `[ 5520.725060]: SWEET KEYS: key=2 down=1 pon=0x2 gpio4:c110=0x1`
    `[ 5520.746445]: SWEET KEYS: key=2 down=0 pon=0 gpio4:c110=0x1`
    `key=2` (Volume Down) fired `down=1` and `down=0` within ~21 ms without any physical touch on the phone.
  - **Why screen wakes & volume HUD appears**: iOS receives `kHIDUsage_Csmr_VolumeDecrement` (or `kHIDUsage_Csmr_Power`), which wakes the display from sleep and renders the native volume slider HUD.
  - **Why Auto-Lock (30s) does not sleep**: Every HID key event dispatches to `IOHIDEventSystem` / backboardd, which calls `IOPMrootDomain::reportUserActivity()`, resetting the idle timer (confirmed via `ioreg -r -c IOPMrootDomain`: `"System Idle Seconds" = 3`). Because phantom key events recur and reset the activity countdown, the 30-second idle timer never reaches expiration.
- **FIX IMPLEMENTED (Patch 0150, committed `154288d`, boot image `shim/out/shim-local-0150-debounce.img`)**:
  - `sweet_keys.c`: added 3-sample 60 ms debouncer (`KEYS_DEBOUNCE_TICKS 3`) in `keys_thread`. Transient SPMI single-tick glitches (like the 21 ms `pon=0x2` spike) are discarded. Keys only trigger when sustained >= 60 ms.
  - `sweet_mpss.c`: turned `g_events` (16 KB) into a rolling window log: when room < 256 bytes, discards the oldest half up to newline via `memmove`, ensuring `sysctl kern.sweet_mpss_events` keeps logging indefinitely without truncating after 17 min.
  - Built kernel `shim/out/kernel-0150` via Docker.
  - `tools/mkadt.py`: added `wlan` node under `/arm-io/` (commit `40a050a`), satisfying `com.apple.wifid.plist`'s `_LimitLoadToDeviceTree: wlan\marconi-wifi` so `launchd` auto-loads `wifid` on boot.
  - Packaged AVB-signed image `shim/out/shim-local-0150-debounce.img` (64 MiB, includes kernel-0150, sunfish.adt with wlan node, and sweetfb_fit=1). Ready for test boot when user is present.

Cause (read from code, not yet proven on the device): after a modem crash the "no connection, try again in 5 s" loop only checked g_wlan_crashed, so the service thread kept polling while mpss_restart_cleanup waited only 3 s and then cycled the WCN3990 rails.

Original `shim-local-0148-present.img` brought the GPU up (submission 1..4) in two of two boots. 0148 kernel + the then-current `shim/out/sunfish.adt` failed; 0154 kernel + an ADT regenerated by tools/mkadt.py (no wlan node) failed; 0154 kernel + the ADT extracted from `payload-0148.bin` worked (`shim-local-0155-fulladt.img`).

Do not regenerate it with mkadt.py; the earlier "wlan node for wifid" change (40a050a) only exists in the minimal ADT and is therefore not in effect.

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: panic with wp.regs == 0, patch 0154 (Claude)
- Observed (photo): Kernel data abort at pc wp_poll+0x164 (the inlined wp_repost register store `wp_ce(ce)[0x40/4]`), via wp_scan <- wlan_station_loop <- wlan_thread, with x9 = 0x241000, i.e. the register base was 0. wp.regs is only zero after `memset(&wp, 0, ...)` in the restart path of wlfw_wmi_bringup.

- Patch 0154 (`shim-local-0156-onethread.img`): wlan_start_thread refuses to start while a service thread runs (compare-and-swap on g_wlan_thread_running); wp_poll, wp_send and jn_ce4_send2 do nothing while wp.regs is NULL. Result: 33+ minutes up, GPU up, Wi-Fi 20/20 pings, no crash, no panic. Earlier crashes came within ~1 minute of boot, so this is a meaningful soak, not proof.

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: the data-path stall is not HT-specific (patch 0164, Claude)
- Observed (HT on, `shim-local-0167-ht/0168/0169/0171`): the link works for 30 s to 10 min, then the firmware stops delivering data: no HTT rx indications (0x12) and no TX completions (0x07), `tx_inflight` climbs, pings get 100% loss. WMI echoes, beacons (WMI mgmt rx) and the SMMU stay normal while it lasts (history ring dumped by the new diagnostics; no SMMU FAULT line, no unhandled HTT/WMI message class, no management action frame from the AP). The route watchdog (3 failed router pings -> `kern.sweet_wlan_kick`) rejoins; after some of these the modem restarts ("restarting the WLAN service").
- Observed (control, `shim-local-0173-noht-rxcnt.img`, `wlannoht=1`, same kernel): the same stall without HT, at about 212 s and 357 s ("N TX frames not completed", then link lost / WLAN restart). So the earlier conclusion "HT kills the link, no-HT is stable" was wrong; the no-HT 33-minute runs were lower traffic or luck.
- Observed: the console log (logs/live-20261028.txt, `grep "SWEET MPSS CRASH"`) holds the crash text, which the rolling event log loses at each modem restart: `wlan_process ... dog_hb.c:367: Task starvation: WLAN BE, ping: 4`. That is the same crash the WMM/EDCA fix (0148) was thought to have eliminated; it still occurs after the data path wedges. Read the console log, not the event log, for crash reasons.
- Ruled out: HTT RX ring exhaustion (2439 buffers consumed, every one reposted, `bad 0`, no stall at 2047); HT-specific vdev/peer mode mismatch is fixed anyway (vdev channel mode is now MODE_11NG_HT20 / 11NA_HT20 when the AP has an HT IE and HT is on; before it started the vdev as 11G with an HT peer) but the stall continues; QoS data frames (tid 0, fc 0x88) instead of non-QoS ones broke all TX (no DHCP) and were reverted.
- Pattern, unproven: the stall starts in a burst of several TX frames (54-601 bytes) within ~100 ms with RX in between, then complete silence on the HTT path.
- Patch 0164 (xnu/patches/0164-sweet-wlan-ht20-vdev-stall-diagnostics.patch): HT20 vdev mode, "MGMT rx" log of unanswered AP management frames, history ring of 160 entries without beacons, a dump of it when 4 TX frames stay uncompleted, RX ring counters in the `EW: N s` line.
- Next: find what the firmware's BE task waits for (candidates: a TX descriptor field of the HTT TX_FRM that only some frames get wrong, the CE4 shadow-register write index, the FW dbglog via WMI), test HT vs no-HT only after the wedge is fixed. HT can be enabled now (it is no less stable than no-HT, and the watchdog recovers in ~30 s), but both modes drop the link every few minutes.

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: the data-path stall is a cache-line race in the CE destination rings, patch 0165 (Claude)
- Observed (stall history with frame classes, 3 stalls on HT): the last event before each stall was an HTT rx indication being processed; then no more rx indications and no TX completions, while WMI echoes and beacons (WMI mgmt rx) went on. It happened with light and heavy traffic, with and without HT.
- Cause (inferred from the code, and the fix below removed every stall in a 17-minute soak): a CE destination descriptor is 16 bytes, the hardware writes the received length (`nbytes`) into it, but `wp_sync` is `dc civac` on a 64-byte line. `wp_repost` wrote one descriptor and cleaned the whole line, writing back stale copies of the three neighbours; if the hardware had completed a neighbour in between, its completion was wiped and `wp_poll` waited on it for ever. CE1 (HTT: rx indications and TX completions) is busy, so it was hit; CE2 (WMI) is quiet, so WMI kept working. The firmware then starved ("Task starvation: WLAN BE") and the modem restarted.
- Patch 0165: destination rings are posted four slots at a time (`wp_repost4`: one whole aligned line of free slots, one register write), the initial post is n-4 slots so the write index stays line-aligned.
- Observed on `shim-local-0176-ht-cl4.img` (HT on, kernel 0170): 17+ minutes, 7700 rx frames, 0 stalls, 0 link losses (before: a stall every 0.5-10 minutes). Not yet a multi-hour soak.
- Also in this patch: TX/RX frame classes in the history ring (HIST keys 0x5/0x6/0x7).

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: soak and repeatability after 0165 (Claude)
- Observed on `shim-local-0176-ht-cl4.img` (HT on): 35 min up, 10.7k rx frames, 0% loss to the router, no stall, no crash, no restart. 8 forced rejoins (`sysctl kern.sweet_wlan_kick=1`, 15 s apart): router pingable again after 2-4 s each time.
- Observed: two reboots of the same image: router pingable ~19-20 s after `fastboot boot`; in the following 150 s no "err_qdi" firmware crash in the console log and no "restarting the WLAN service" event. So the first-minute WLAN restart seen on every boot before 0165 did not occur in these two boots (it was probably the same CE ring race; two boots is not proof).
- Not done: multi-hour soak, DHCP retry path (needs a lost ack), 5 GHz / other security types, power save with the screen off, IPv6, Settings integration (Antigravity's steps 16-18).

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: IPv6 on ew0 (Antigravity's step 18), no kernel change (Claude)
- Observed: after a boot ew0 had no IPv6 at all (no link-local address; `ndp -i ew0` said EINVAL) and ue0 neither. Neither `ndp -i ew0 accept_rtadv` nor `ifconfig` can enable router-advertisement handling (the ND6_IFF_ACCEPT_RTADV flag is unused on Darwin; the per-interface switch is the ifnet flag IFEF_ACCEPT_RTADVD, set by the SIOCAUTOCONF_START ioctl).
- Fix, all userland: `userland/net/sweet-autoconf6.c` does what IPConfiguration does for an "IPv6 Automatic" service: SIOCLL_START (attaches IPv6, link-local address; struct in6_aliasreq is 128 bytes) then SIOCAUTOCONF_START (struct in6_ifreq is 288 bytes; the ioctl numbers encode the size). The kernel's own router solicitation is not answered, so `tools/sweet-wlan-route.sh` also runs `rtsol ew0` for 8 s after the link comes up (and every ~30 s while no router is known), then installs an unscoped default route via the advertised router (`route -n add -inet6 default fe80::1%ew0`; the kernel's own default for ew0 is interface-scoped and gives "No route to host").
- Observed after a clean reboot with the final script: global SLAAC addresses (2a01:5a8:...: a secured and a temporary one), IPv6 default route via ew0, 3/3 pings to 2001:4860:4860::8888 at ~11 ms, IPv4 unchanged. Installed on the phone: /var/jb/usr/bin/sweet-autoconf6, /var/root/sweet-wlan-route.sh (the daemon com.sweet.wlan runs it).
- Note: the phone has no pkill, awk or strings in ssh's PATH; `rtsol` needs a link-local address first (EINVAL otherwise). DHCPv6 is not done (SLAAC only, which is what the router offers).

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: throughput was limited by the legacy-rate encoding, patch 0167 (Claude)
- Observed (new tools `userland/net/sweet-bw.c` on the phone + `tools/sweet-bw-server.py` on the PC, TCP over Wi-Fi, HT on, RSSI about -70 dBm): TX 0.6 Mbit/s and RX 9-16 Mbit/s; ping RTT grew ~8 us per byte (5 ms for 56 B, 16 ms for 1400 B), i.e. the firmware transmitted at about 1 Mbit/s.
- Cause (A/B-confirmed): `jn_peer_assoc` set bit 7 on the four CCK rates (1, 2, 5.5, 11) as if it were the 802.11 basic-rate flag. ath10k's `ath10k_mac_bitrate_to_rate` sets bit 7 only for 5.5 Mbit/s (the odd one); the firmware's rate control took the other CCK rates as invalid/lowest and stayed at the bottom of the table.
- Patch 0167: bit 7 only on 5.5 Mbit/s (and the 11B/11G phy-mode guess for non-HT peers now looks at the first rate being CCK instead of bit 7). Also adds `kern.sweet_wlan_vparam` = (vdev param id << 24) | value, which sets a WMI vdev parameter on the running station: `0x1b000004` fixed rate OFDM 24 Mbit/s, `0x1b000207` HT MCS7, `0x1b000000` back to rate control (vdev param ids from wmi-tlv.h: FIXED_RATE 0x1b, SGI 0x1c, LDPC 0x1d, TX_STBC 0x1e, RX_STBC 0x1f, NSS 0x22, MAX_RATE 0x3d).
- Observed after the fix (`shim-local-0179-vparam.img`, 5 minutes up, three runs): TX 15-21 Mbit/s, RX 41-45 Mbit/s, ping(1400 B) min 4.9 ms. Same-uptime A/B on the old kernel (0177): TX 0.6-0.7, RX 9-16. Forcing a fixed higher rate is worse here (OFDM 54 or MCS7: TX 6 Mbit/s, RX 40), so rate control picks well at this signal level.
- Left: RSSI to the router is only about -70 dBm (the phone is not next to it); 5 GHz and other security types cannot be tested with the networks in range (only others' 5 GHz APs, ch 40/48, at -82/-86 dBm).

Not yet investigated: probably the IGTK KDE or the RSN capabilities (MFPC) in our RSN IE.

- Observed (kernel-0169-insert = 0168 + 0169, phone): the user's <HOTSPOT> hotspot (channel 112, 5560 MHz, "WPA2 WPA3" mixed, HT20 mode 4 in our assoc) joins with our WPA2-PSK supplicant: auth/assoc OK, 4-way handshake with a 163-byte message 3 (the home router's is 155), keys installed, DHCP 10.179.238.184 via router 10.179.238.70, gateway ping 3.6-13 ms, internet ping 8-20 ms. ### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: Settings integration step 1, Apple's IO80211 library works against ew0 (patch 0170, Claude)
- Finding (disassembly of IO80211.framework from the dyld shared cache, extracted with `ipsw dyld extract`, in research/wifi/dylibs/, git-ignored; table of the 418 request types in research/wifi/apple80211_ioc_table.txt): Apple80211GetIfListCopy = getifaddrs + `SIOCGIFMEDIA` and lists only interfaces whose media type is IEEE 802.11 (`(media & 0xe0) == 0x80`); Apple80211BindToInterface finds an IO80211Controller in IOKit and, if there is none ("trying dk matching"), falls back to plain `SIOCGA80211` (`_IOWR('i',201, struct apple80211req)`, 40 bytes) / `SIOCSA80211` (`_IOW('i',200,...)`) ioctls on the interface, after checking the IORegistry entry found with `IOServiceGetMatchingService({IOPropertyMatch: {IOInterfaceName: "ew0"}})` has `IO80211InterfaceRole` = "Infrastructure". So no IO80211Controller is needed.
- Patch 0170: `iokit/Kernel/arm/SweetWiFi.cpp` (class IO80211Interface stand-in with IOInterfaceName / BSD Name / IO80211InterfaceRole / ...), published from `ew_if_create`; ew0 media IFM_IEEE80211; `ew_a80211()` in sweet_mpss.c answers the ioctls (struct layouts found with marker values and from the library's loads): CARD_CAPABILITIES 12 (13 bytes), POWER 19 {version,num_radios,state[4]}, STATE 13 / OP_MODE 15 / RATE 8 (value in req_val, req_len 0), SSID 1 {version,len,bytes}, BSSID 9 (bare 6 bytes), CHANNEL 4 {version,{version,channel,flags}}, PHY_MODE 14 {version,supported,active}, RSSI 16 / NOISE 17 (52 bytes: {version,num_radios,unit,ctl[4],ctl_agr,ext[4],ext_agr}), COUNTRY_CODE 51, DRIVER/HARDWARE_VERSION 43/44 (C string from offset 0), SUPPORTED_CHANNELS 27, SCAN_REQ 10 (set: posts event SCAN_DONE = 10) and SCAN_RESULT 11 (get: next result from the scan cache, errno 5 after the last; record layout in the code). Events are kernel events vendor 1, class 6, subclass 1: event code, then the interface name (16 bytes) and a payload (codes from itlwm's list: 1 power, 2 ssid, 3 bssid, 4 link, 9 assoc done, 10 scan done). Unhandled requests are logged as `A80211:` lines in `kern.sweet_mpss_events`.
- Observed (`shim-local-0187-scan2.img`, probe `userland/net/sweet-a80211-probe.c`): Open/Bind ew0 OK; GetPower on; GetInfoCopy returns SSID, BSSID, channel, RSSI, noise, PHY mode, state 4, rate; Apple80211Scan returns all networks with SSID, BSSID, channel, RSSI, RSN/WPA info, WPS, QBSS, country code (data from our scan cache, so results are those of the last join-time scan).
- Not done: rescan on request (SCAN_REQ only replays the cache), ASSOCIATE (20) / DISASSOCIATE (22) from Settings, link/power/ssid events, wifid started by launchd (its plist has `_LimitLoadToDeviceTree = [wlan, marconi-wifi]`; original saved in research/wifi/com.apple.wifid.plist.orig), Settings itself not looked at yet.

### 2026-10-28 Wi-Fi: wifid blocked SpringBoard at boot (Apple logo); fix `Plugin:InterfaceNamer` (Claude)
- Setup: launchd on this system reads daemons from the cache /System/Library/xpc/launchd.plist (boot-arg launchd_unsecure_cache=1), not from the individual plists. wifid's entry there has `_LimitLoadToDeviceTree = [wlan, marconi-wifi]`, which is why it never started. Originals: /var/root/launchd.plist.orig on the phone and research/wifi/launchd.plist.orig (git-ignored); the edited cache (key removed) is /var/root/launchd.plist.wifid-enabled and is installed now.
- Observed: with wifid running (pid 43) SpringBoard never drew its first frame (screen stays on the Apple logo; SpringBoard, backboardd, ssh all fine); `launchctl bootout system/com.apple.wifid` made the lock screen appear at once.
- Cause (crash reports forced with `kill -ABRT`, research/wifi/springboard-hang.ips and wifid-hang.ips): SpringBoard's main thread sits in `MobileWiFi wifi_manager_open` <- `WiFiManagerClientCreate` (mach_msg to wifid, no reply). In wifid, `com.apple.wifid.managerQueue` is blocked in SystemConfiguration `_SCNetworkInterfaceCopyAllWithPreferences` -> dispatch_once, whose owner thread is in `____wait_for_IOKit_to_quiesce_block_invoke` -> `SCDynamicStoreNotifyWait`: SC waits until the store key `Plugin:InterfaceNamer` (written by configd's InterfaceNamer plugin, which does not run here) holds `*QUIET*`, `*COMPLETE*` or `*TIMEOUT*` ("Waiting for IOKit to quiesce (or timeout)").
- Fix: `userland/net/sweet-ifnamer.c` publishes `Plugin:InterfaceNamer = {*QUIET* = true}` (installed as /var/jb/usr/bin/sweet-ifnamer); `tools/sweet-wlan-route.sh` runs it first thing at boot. Observed after a reboot with wifid enabled in the cache: SpringBoard reaches the home screen, wifid stays up.
- Mistake to avoid: `kill -ABRT` on SpringBoard puts the jailbreak into safe mode (tweaks disabled) until the user exits it.
- Open problem (not understood): twice (once ~3 minutes after wifid first ran with the UI up, once ~14 minutes after boot) the USB network to the PC (ue0 / 192.168.7.x) and with it the console log stopped working while the phone stayed fully alive: ue0 was UP with its address on the phone, the PC saw the interface, `ping 192.168.7.2` failed; a reboot restored it. ssh over Wi-Fi (192.168.1.14, key-less root with `-o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null`) kept working, so use it when USB is gone.
- Observed: Wi-Fi throughput fell to ~0.3 Mbit/s and RTT to 150-470 ms after a data-path stall ("10 TX frames not completed" at 249 s, while wifid had just been started and the phone was downloading); `sysctl kern.sweet_wlan_vparam=$((0x1b000004))` (fixed OFDM 24) and then `0x1b000000` (back to rate control) restored 41 Mbit/s down / 20 up: the firmware's rate control had been stuck at a low rate. Cause unknown; a workaround would be to detect a low rate (peer stats) and do that toggle.
